# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-04

Expanded creative-AI discovery adds astronomical imaging and tactile graphics while completing supported earlier pending guides. Every featured software grant is read in full; model, service and media terms remain separate. Documentation was reviewed without installing or running discovered apps. No unchanged previously completed guide is recycled as a new edition finding. This edition contains 52 first full guides and 9 additional license-screened pending guides. 20 guides complete previously screened entries; one additional guide uses the current canonical kaampi Write identity after an earlier StoryBook alias. Existing unchanged Blender and Motion Anything canonical projects are not repeated from redirect/mirror candidates. Current per-tool source checks include bounded release, commit and support captures; unresolved independent use, model terms and physical/output validation remain explicit. A closed PR or current README is not proof of a working end-to-end installation. Verified collection: 704/704 repository queries attempted; 704 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 121 bounded/truncated. All 18 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Separate from successful search queries, 26 watchlist fetches and 3 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

Tactile SVGDreamer is additionally retained as a MIT-screened pending guide; its stated reward-file path was unavailable and physical legibility is not established. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22674) [Source 2](https://github.com/shljessie/tactile-svgdreamer) |

## Search and review counts

85 creative fields; 704/704 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 29179 distinct source candidates and 862 model leads. 52 detailed profiles and 9 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 121 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 2 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Current full primary READMEs were read for Embedded Amp Model and AudioStellar, and LibreTerrain was captured for comparison. Synthetic amp targets and a validated C port do not prove an on-device pedal; AudioStellar’s current macOS build instructions remain ToDo. Source-host coverage is bounded and is not a new grant clearance. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ar39907/embedded-amp-model/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ayrsd/audiostellar/-/raw/audiostellar2/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/libreterrain/libreterrain/-/raw/main/README.md)
- **codeberg** · gap: The live discovery attempt was denied by robots restrictions. No alternate route was used to bypass that restriction. No usable current primary page or newly qualified project was established through the attempt.
- **sourcehut** · gap: The live discovery attempt was denied by robots restrictions. Historical sources remain preserved, but are not represented as newly accessed evidence. This is an explicit ecosystem access gap, not absence of creative AI projects.
- **packages** · searched: The current NuGet TensorStack.Media version index was captured and compared with the source overview. Package versions alone do not document a reproducible creative AI pipeline; the SDK stays pending a deeper usage/setup review. [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json) [Source](https://github.com/saddam213/TensorStack)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Current primary source reviews cover code-agent paper animation and presentation packages, local-model integration and editor extensions. Their code licenses do not license host agents, proprietary APIs or media. An older Blender add-on alias redirects to an already profiled canonical project, so it is not recycled as a new tool. [Source](https://github.com/OpenRootdev/paper-unfold-mesh) [Source](https://github.com/OpenRootdev/paper-unfold-frames) [Source](https://github.com/AlienZhang1996/Academic-PPT-Workflow) [Source](https://github.com/hitkeeen/blender-ai-addon)
- **project-sites** · searched: Fresh primary-source attempts supplement all 85 fields through 77 unique initial web targets: 75 HTTP successes and two failures. Additional targeted sites and GitLab/package sources were captured separately. Some successful responses are limited shells or bounded PDFs. OpenSlides SSL and the museum 404 remain gaps. [Source](https://somacad.app/) [Source](https://solidcraft.io/) [Source](https://majmichu1.github.io/Astraios/) [Source](https://tactilenet.github.io/)
- **research-code** · searched: The current AstroSURE and tactile-graphics papers expand discovery beyond the earlier fields. Separate complete-grant reviews support Astraios, Muphrid, Noise2Noise Astro and TactileNet. ASTRA-SR has non-commercial code restrictions; missing Text2TactileGraphics licensing is not repaired by its paper’s physical results. [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.16793) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22674) [Source](https://github.com/I2WM/ASTRA-SR) [Source](https://github.com/RuihanGao/Text2TactileGraphics-Code)
- **international** · searched: Current Chinese and Vietnamese creative-workflow documentation was read alongside English sources, including paper-collage production, MuseLab notation and 2Anh Studio. A Russian Blender-add-on source was inspected but its canonical project was already profiled. Source language, popularity and origin do not establish quality. [Source](https://github.com/yzsp-studio/paper-collage-explainer-generator) [Source](https://github.com/z-biz-tool/z-biz-tool-music) [Source](https://github.com/luonghaianh1208/2anh-studio) [Source](https://github.com/hitkeeen/blender-ai-addon)

Scope: Process deep-sky or planetary captures with local learned denoising, then compare the result with classical processing before exporting an astronomical image.

This label concerns the present application, not astrophotography research generally. https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios/blob/main/README.md https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios/blob/main/docs/installation.md https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios/blob/main/docs/project-facts.md https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios/blob/main/NOTICE https://majmichu1.github.io/Astraios/

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Current alpha workflows are expected to contain bugs. AI Sharpen has no released trained model and falls back to classical deconvolution; built-in star removal is morphological. NVIDIA CUDA is maintainer-tested on one laptop, not GPU CI. No CR2/NEF/ARW/DNG support. Selecting astrometry.net uploads a mono FITS copy; local processing alone does not describe that optional route.

### How it uses AI

A desktop astrophotography processor with bundled Noise2Self U-Net denoising and optional Real-ESRGAN super-resolution alongside conventional calibration, alignment and stacking. 2. Use the official platform release: Windows x64 installer, Linux x86_64 installer/AppImage, or the macOS installer. Initial setup downloads the matching PyTorch build. Source development uses Python 3.11–3.14 and Poetry.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios.git
```


```sh
cd Astraios
```


```sh
poetry install --with dev
```


```sh
poetry run astraios
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios/blob/main/docs/installation.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios/blob/main/docs/project-facts.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/majmichu1/Astraios/blob/main/NOTICE) [Source 5](https://majmichu1.github.io/Astraios/)

1. Open FITS, XISF, TIFF or another documented format; convert camera RAW files first.
2. Use the guided processing workflow to calibrate/register/stack a small capture set.
3. Compare bundled AI Denoise against classical alternatives; inspect faint structures and stars.
4. Export FITS, XISF, TIFF or PNG, retaining the original data.

### How it uses AI

### Introduction

A LangGraph LLM agent orchestrates Siril, GraXpert, StarNet2 and image-analysis libraries for astrophotography post-processing, producing and measuring variants with human approval gates. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jomonsugi/muphrid/blob/main/README.md)



Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jomonsugi/muphrid/blob/main/README.md)

[Source](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Introduction

A PyTorch U-Net notebook learns astronomical image denoising from independently corrupted synthetic image pairs, then demonstrates tiled inference on a real M101 capture. [Source 1](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### What it is good for

Study the difference between synthetic denoising metrics and the visual behavior of a learned filter on real astrophotography. [Source 1](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Demo & examples

Author-reported synthetic validation improves PSNR from 18.2 to 26.9 dB and SSIM from 0.27 to 0.78. A real M101 example uses 256-pixel tiles with 32-pixel overlap; no independent replication was established. [Source 1](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Install

Use the linked Colab notebook or install the repository requirements locally and open Noise2Noise_Astro_2.ipynb with Jupyter. [Source 1](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/requirements.txt)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the linked Colab notebook or install the repository requirements locally and open Noise2Noise_Astro_2.ipynb with Jupyter.

```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
jupyter notebook Noise2Noise_Astro_2.ipynb
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/astroedo/Noise2noise-astro/blob/main/requirements.txt)

1. Start with the notebook training/validation example.
2. In the user-image section set image_path to your own image.
3. Review tiled output and the documented 0.6 blend against the original.
4. Check faint features and dense star fields before retaining a processed image.

- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A dated bounded snapshot of up to five releases, ten commits and fifteen updated support items was archived and inspected alongside the current guide. This does not establish sustained workflow support or a complete unresolved-blocker audit.

The current known-issues guide says chat history is daemon-memory-only, clips lack an in-point for proper split/left-trim behavior, and long previews can drift between audio and image clocks. Export timing is separately aligned by FFmpeg. WebGL export uses SwiftShader and can differ from GPU preview; broad cross-device determinism remains unverified. ### How it uses AI

A browser/Tauri music workspace optionally asks an LLM for ABC notation, validates a strict subset into an internal score and synchronizes staff, numbered notation, piano-roll and mixer views. [Source](https://github.com/z-biz-tool/z-biz-tool-music/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A browser/Tauri music workspace optionally asks an LLM for ABC notation, validates a strict subset into an internal score and synchronizes staff, numbered notation, piano-roll and mixer views. [Source 1](https://github.com/z-biz-tool/z-biz-tool-music/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Generate or develop a short score, edit notes in a piano roll and export MIDI, MusicXML, ABC, project JSON or rendered WAV. [Source 1](https://github.com/z-biz-tool/z-biz-tool-music/blob/main/README.md)

Use Node 22.18+ to install dependencies and run the browser development server at localhost:5200. Desktop development additionally uses the Tauri toolchain; configure a provider only for AI composition. [Source 1](https://github.com/z-biz-tool/z-biz-tool-music/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Node 22.18+ to install dependencies and run the browser development server at localhost:5200. Desktop development additionally uses the Tauri toolchain; configure a provider only for AI composition.

```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run dev
```


```sh

```

```sh
git clone https://github.com/luonghaianh1208/2anh-studio.git
```


### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/luonghaianh1208/2anh-studio/blob/main/README.md)

2. Answer the initial brief questions and review generated content.
3. For video inspect each illustrated narration beat and captions.
4. Check the final editable slides or MP4 and retain source projects.

## HiSTF Mamba

Motion capture & character animation · 3D, reconstruction & assets · Computational art & creative coding

The repository was created on 2026-10-20. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Study or train a HumanML3D/KIT-ML motion model, with an explicit distinction between the reconstructed implementation and the paper checkpoint.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-04. No local execution implied.

Why Experimental: The present reconstruction has no matching released pretrained checkpoint, an unresolved model-size discrepancy and no reproduced paper benchmark; generation needs a compatible trained/recovered model. https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/README.md https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The paper reports FID results, but the current source has not reproduced them. A matching pretrained checkpoint is not yet available, so generation examples are conditional on training/recovering matching weights. [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Create a separate Python 3.9 environment, install the documented PyTorch 2.0.1 CUDA 11.8 stack and requirements, then build the vendored causal-conv1d/custom Mamba backend. Prepare the separately governed datasets and evaluator assets. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A dated bounded snapshot of up to five releases, ten commits and fifteen updated support items was archived and inspected alongside the current guide. This does not establish sustained workflow support or a complete unresolved-blocker audit.
- **Independent use · unknown:** The examined current documentation and bounded support snapshot did not establish independent first-hand reproduction of this complete scoped workflow. Owner demos, automated PRs, feature proposals and partial tests are not treated as that proof.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT author-owned code; vendored upstream components, HumanML3D/KIT-ML data, GloVe and evaluator assets have separate terms. Training/inference compute and separately governed data/evaluation assets. [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The reconstructed 15.53M-parameter model differs from the paper’s 23.95M and the discrepancy is unresolved. Sequences above 196 frames are rejected. Historical environment versions and CPU tests are not proof of current CUDA compilation or reproduced paper quality.

### How it uses AI

A source reconstruction of a text-to-motion diffusion model combines part/whole-body spatial modeling, bidirectional temporal processing and dynamic fusion. A source reconstruction of a text-to-motion diffusion model combines part/whole-body spatial modeling, bidirectional temporal processing and dynamic fusion. [Source 1](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md)

Study or train a HumanML3D/KIT-ML motion model, with an explicit distinction between the reconstructed implementation and the paper checkpoint. ### Install

Create a separate Python 3.9 environment, install the documented PyTorch 2.0.1 CUDA 11.8 stack and requirements, then build the vendored causal-conv1d/custom Mamba backend. Prepare the separately governed datasets and evaluator assets. [Source 1](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create a separate Python 3.9 environment, install the documented PyTorch 2.0.1 CUDA 11.8 stack and requirements, then build the vendored causal-conv1d/custom Mamba backend. Prepare the separately governed datasets and evaluator assets.

```sh
conda create -n histfmamba python=3.9 -y
```


```sh
conda activate histfmamba
```


```sh
python -m pip install torch==2.0.1 torchvision==0.15.2 torchaudio==2.0.2 --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu118
```


```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
bash tools/install_backend.sh
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md)

1. Check the custom backend with tools/check_environment.py.
2. Prepare HumanML3D or KIT-ML, normalization files and evaluator assets, then run preprocessing.
3. Train the documented matching configuration, or recover a compatible checkpoint before attempting inference.
4. Generate a short motion request, inspect joint arrays and keep the checkpoint/configuration pair together.

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/SETUP.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/chorl0229/HiSTFmamba/blob/main/docs/USAGE.md)

- **Hardware:** The training example selects two devices and bf16-mixed for 350 epochs. Minimum GPU model/VRAM, system RAM and total disk are not documented. These training settings are examples, not verified minima.
- **Software:** Python 3.9, PyTorch 2.0.1/torchvision 0.15.2/torchaudio 2.0.2, CUDA 11.8 toolkit with nvcc and compatible C++ compiler. Custom source-built scan backends are required; unrelated Mamba wheels are not substitutes.
- **Platforms:** A working NVIDIA/CUDA environment is required; no tested OS-version matrix or CPU/MPS end-to-end generation route is established.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Tested on only one voice; inputs stopping below 7 kHz remain untested. The generated band can be brighter than a real speaker and briefly overshoot the reference ceiling. Without a reference there is no ceiling, only gain/roll-off controls.

### How it uses AI

A local speech bandwidth-extension utility uses LavaSR to guess missing high-frequency content, then re-levels it against the input and optionally caps it using a real reference voice. [Source](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A local speech bandwidth-extension utility uses LavaSR to guess missing high-frequency content, then re-levels it against the input and optionally caps it using a real reference voice. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Compare raw and controlled bandwidth extension for a band-limited synthetic voice without treating generated high frequencies as recovered original information. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

Owner measurements cover 77 Chinese lines from one IndexTTS voice. Reference-capped output and raw model output are compared; one-voice measurements do not establish multilingual or speaker-general quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Create a Python 3.10+ environment, install a suitable PyTorch/torchaudio build and install the project from Git. The first run obtains two fixed-revision model files and checks their hashes. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create a Python 3.10+ environment, install a suitable PyTorch/torchaudio build and install the project from Git. The first run obtains two fixed-revision model files and checks their hashes.

```sh
python -m venv .venv
```


```sh
pip install torch torchaudio --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu
```


```sh
pip install "synchain-bwe @ git+https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe"
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

1. Run synchain-bwe detect voice.wav to inspect the cutoff.
2. Process a short file, optionally providing a clean authorized full-band reference with -r.
3. Compare raw, capped and original audio; inspect silence and sibilants.
4. Retain the report and use band-only output when layering it under the original at 48 kHz.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Model download is about 56 MB. Owner timing uses a Ryzen 9 9950X with eight threads at about 15× realtime; that is not a minimum or cross-machine guarantee. RAM/VRAM/total disk minima are undocumented.
- **Software:** Python >=3.10, Git, PyTorch and torchaudio; pinned Vocos fork dependency and LavaSR enhancer weights.
- **Platforms:** Windows and macOS/Linux environment-activation routes are documented; no minimum OS-version matrix is supplied.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT wrapper code. The README notes LavaSR LICENSE/model-card Apache-2.0 versus package-metadata MIT wording; these underlying terms need their own review. Vocos uses a separately pinned MIT fork.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT wrapper code. The README notes LavaSR LICENSE/model-card Apache-2.0 versus package-metadata MIT wording; these underlying terms need their own review. Vocos uses a separately pinned MIT fork.
- **Cost:** Local inference; initial Hugging Face download. Audio is documented to remain local.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Tested on only one voice; inputs stopping below 7 kHz remain untested. The generated band can be brighter than a real speaker and briefly overshoot the reference ceiling. Without a reference there is no ceiling, only gain/roll-off controls. [Source 1](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/synchain-oss/synchain-bwe/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation and issue closure alone do not prove a reproduced installation or resolve every older limitation. [Source](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/issues/131) [Source](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/issues/143) [Source](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/issues/144)
- **Independent use · unknown:** The examined current documentation and bounded support snapshot did not establish independent first-hand reproduction of this complete scoped workflow. Owner demos, automated PRs, feature proposals and partial tests are not treated as that proof.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Apache-2.0 project grant. build123d/OpenCascade and the chosen coding-agent/model account have separate terms; local CAD execution does not make agent inference local. Local CAD compute plus the user’s Claude Code/model access. [Source](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. There is no manual sketcher/feature tree; Python is the model. Scan dimensions are approximate. The author expects rough edges in this early app. Generated checks are not engineering certification.

### How it uses AI

An Electron/build123d workbench connects Claude Code to model views, face references and B-rep measurements so an agent can edit Python CAD and rebuild it on save. [Source](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An Electron/build123d workbench connects Claude Code to model views, face references and B-rep measurements so an agent can edit Python CAD and rebuild it on save. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Iterate a parametric part by pinning a request to a specific face or measured wall, then reviewing the rebuilt geometry and dimensions. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

Owner screenshots and a demo GIF show an airbox in a scan, marked dimensions and an agent-driven edit. Those examples do not certify mechanical fit, material strength or printability. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

On Linux/macOS install Node >=22.12, pnpm 10, uv and a logged-in Claude Code on PATH. Clone, install dependencies and launch scripts/torno with a workspace folder. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. On Linux/macOS install Node >=22.12, pnpm 10, uv and a logged-in Claude Code on PATH. Clone, install dependencies and launch scripts/torno with a workspace folder.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno.git
```


```sh
cd torno
```


```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
scripts/torno ~/projects
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open the temporary example copy before using original projects.
2. Pin or measure a face/edge and give the agent a dimension/change request.
3. Inspect the Python rebuild, last-good geometry and pass/fail measurement.
4. Export STEP/STL/3MF, an HTML view, PNG or a true-scale section SVG/PDF as appropriate; independently check dimensions and fabrication constraints.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The initial private Python/build123d environment is about 900 MB; RAM/VRAM and total project/build-storage minima are not specified.
- **Software:** Node >=22.12, pnpm 10, uv, Claude Code; private Python 3.11/3.12 environment with build123d unless a project supplies its own environment. Current first-build environment also includes bd_warehouse; Linux AppImage/tar.gz and ad-hoc-signed, non-notarized macOS packaging commands are documented.
- **Platforms:** Linux and macOS documented. Windows is explicitly unsupported because the launcher, terminal and login-shell PATH are Unix-specific; WSL2 is untried.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 project grant. build123d/OpenCascade and the chosen coding-agent/model account have separate terms; local CAD execution does not make agent inference local.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 project grant. build123d/OpenCascade and the chosen coding-agent/model account have separate terms; local CAD execution does not make agent inference local.
- **Cost:** Local CAD compute plus the user’s Claude Code/model access.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Scan dimensions remain approximate. Current documentation favors measured prismatic parts such as brackets/enclosures; organic verbal descriptions, hand-written gears/threads, photo-derived dimensions and larger touching assemblies are weaker workflows. Windows is unsupported and WSL2 untried. Last-good geometry is not a general file-undo guarantee, and generated checks are not engineering certification. [Source 1](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/issues/131) [Source 3](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/issues/143) [Source 4](https://github.com/luisg0nc/torno/issues/144)

### Introduction

Use the documented Ubuntu/Python/CUDA reference stack; install modified differentiable rasterization, simple-knn, nvdiffrast, kiuikit and requirements, and separately follow the Real-ESRGAN setup. [Source 1](https://github.com/YaNLlan-ljb/ContrastiveGaussian/blob/main/README.md)

```sh
conda create -n contrastivegaussian python=3.8
```



Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The README’s complete-coverage and zero-latency claims are not independently established. Keyframe extraction does not guarantee faithful unseen geometry. Actual generation is remote and must not be described as local photogrammetry.

### How it uses AI

A FastAPI relay extracts orbit-video frames, but the current AI route submits only the first selected frame to Tripo image_to_model before importing a returned GLB into Blender. The keyless source builds a conventional brightness-relief OBJ, not learned 3D reconstruction. [Source](https://github.com/aashu91/video-to-blender/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/aashu91/video-to-blender/blob/main/server.py)




2. Distinguish the keyless grayscale-relief fallback from actual remote AI generation; the README’s reference-asset wording is outdated relative to the inspected source.
3. Record/upload an orbit clip up to 120 seconds and inspect extracted views.
4. Review the generated asset’s hidden surfaces, scale and material before using it in a scene.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/aashu91/video-to-blender/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python >=3.10, FastAPI >=0.110-class stack, OpenCV/NumPy/Pillow/HTTPX, Blender 3.0+/4.x and Tripo3D API v2. HTTPS tunnel/service needed for remote mobile camera use.
- **Platforms:** Blender 3.x/4.x and mobile browser capture described; minimum desktop/mobile OS versions and device support are not established.

### Limitations

### Introduction

### How it uses AI

### Introduction

A self-hosted chat-style studio queues ComfyUI generation/transcription jobs on an allocated Google Colab L4 runtime, copying results back before releasing the remote GPU. [Source 1](https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Access multiple creative model workflows from a local/server UI when the documented remote L4 route suits the available account and budget. [Source 1](https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The current README adds owner UI screenshots for GPU startup, speech/voice cloning and mobile layout, alongside workflow/model budgets. These are owner demonstrations; mocked UI checks are distinct from a paid-GPU smoke test or independent cross-model quality review. [Source 1](https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Linux/WSL2 with curl, SSH, tar and Python, then clone and run the documented installer and launcher. Complete the Google Colab account sign-in separately; Docker is another documented route. [Source 1](https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Linux/WSL2 with curl, SSH, tar and Python, then clone and run the documented installer and launcher. Complete the Google Colab account sign-in separately; Docker is another documented route.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio.git
```


```sh
cd colab-studio
```


```sh
./install.sh
```


```sh
./run.sh
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open localhost:8190 and confirm account balance and GPU allocation behavior.
2. Choose one mode; the first job allocates the L4 and downloads its compatible models.
3. Inspect the returned artifact and confirm it is retained locally.
4. Release the runtime or verify the configured idle release; check selected model rights before commercial use.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Remote NVIDIA L4 with 24 GB VRAM is the documented route. Models are quantized to fit it. Local host RAM/storage minima and per-mode total download requirements are not published in the README.
- **Software:** Linux or WSL2, curl/ssh/tar/python3; installer provisions uv, Python 3.12 and Colab CLI. Google account with paid compute units; optional Node >=20 for UI builds and authorized HF access for gated models.
- **Platforms:** Linux and Windows WSL2 documented; macOS explicitly untested. The remote workflows support only Colab L4.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/berkeduruu/colab-studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Weights:** AGPL-3.0 studio code. Colab, ComfyUI/plugins, cached third-party wheels and all image/video/music/voice/3D models retain separate terms; model replacement is architecture-specific.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows AGPL-3.0. AGPL-3.0 studio code. Colab, ComfyUI/plugins, cached third-party wheels and all image/video/music/voice/3D models retain separate terms; model replacement is architecture-specific.
- **Cost:** Colab compute units while the runtime is allocated, plus any separately governed model/service resources; idle release is a cost-control mechanism, not zero cost.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official implementation links its NeurIPS paper and codec-matrix tests. Functional decoding tests do not establish superior rate/distortion or perceptual quality. [Source](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use Python 3.10 and install the source package with test extras. Dependencies include PyTorch 2.2.2 and CompressAI 1.2.6; install the appropriate CUDA build first if using GPU, or select CPU. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A dated bounded snapshot of up to five releases, ten commits and fifteen updated support items was archived and inspected alongside the current guide. This does not establish sustained workflow support or a complete unresolved-blocker audit.

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** Complete BSD-3-Clause code license reviewed. TCM, pretrained CompressAI weights and all training/test images have separate terms; models and datasets are not bundled. Local/rented training/inference compute and separate model/data storage. [Source](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Decoder needs matching model/head files whose sizes are excluded from image bpp. LFC1 streams require re-encoding to LFC2. Numerical and real coding can use different padding, so comparisons need matched conventions.

### How it uses AI

A PyTorch neural-image-compression implementation trains learnable eight-dimensional lattice routes and fits a probability head for actual image bitstream encoding/decoding. [Source](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A PyTorch neural-image-compression implementation trains learnable eight-dimensional lattice routes and fits a probability head for actual image bitstream encoding/decoding. [Source 1](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Research a learned image codec with explicit rate/distortion and real-file measurements rather than substituting estimated bitrate for encoded size. [Source 1](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The official implementation links its NeurIPS paper and codec-matrix tests. Functional decoding tests do not establish superior rate/distortion or perceptual quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use Python 3.10 and install the source package with test extras. Dependencies include PyTorch 2.2.2 and CompressAI 1.2.6; install the appropriate CUDA build first if using GPU, or select CPU. [Source 1](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use Python 3.10 and install the source package with test extras. Dependencies include PyTorch 2.2.2 and CompressAI 1.2.6; install the appropriate CUDA build first if using GPU, or select CPU.

```sh
python -m pip install -e '.[test]'
```


```sh
olvq --help
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)

1. Prepare training images at least as large as the crop size, default 256×256.
2. Train a chosen codec/route and fit its probability head using training images only.
3. Encode an input PNG with the matching checkpoint/head and decode the resulting .olvq file.
4. Compare reconstruction and actual file bpp, preserving the same device/software profile.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/xzhang9308/OLVQ/blob/main/README.md)

- **Platforms:** CPU and CUDA device routes documented; exact host OS compatibility is not published.

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Magonox/omarchy-goop/blob/main/README.md)

1. Use the setup card and choose the desired access/approval level.
2. Select a non-sensitive page and request a short 3D explanation.
3. Approve the lesson request and inspect geometry, narration and factual content.
4. Close the lesson and review settings; optional page reading and voice require their own setup.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Magonox/omarchy-goop/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Optional voice setup is about 450 MB. The owner cites around 400 MB loaded recognizer memory and 260 MB for a lesson as measurements, not system minima. Minimum RAM/GPU/total disk are not established.
- **Software:** Omarchy/Hyprland, Claude Code, grim; optional Python 3/Piper/Moonshine/paplay/parecord. 3D lessons use qt6-quick3d and Deno. Other compatible providers can serve chat, but the documented lesson planner currently uses Claude.
- **Platforms:** Omarchy Linux with Hyprland. No macOS/Windows equivalent is documented.

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Magonox/omarchy-goop/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Magonox/omarchy-goop/blob/main/README.md)

- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT original plugin code. Piper GPL-3.0-or-later, voice/model terms, Moonshine, Qt/Deno and the selected agent/provider retain separate grants.

- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT app code and ForgePix-trained restoration weights as documented by the model card. NAFNet/BasicSR notices, public astronomical data, optional external models/tools and provider accounts remain separate. Local compute/storage; optional model APIs, external astronomy software and remote GPU hosts have separate costs. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The ordinary automatic stacker is deterministic and does not silently enable neural restoration. [Source](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.Core/Inference/OnnxInferenceEngine.cs) [Source](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.UI/AstroCounter.UI.csproj) [Source](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.Core/AstroCounter.Core.csproj) [Source](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/scripts/train.py)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The source uses 640×640 neural tiles, but a tile size is not a minimum VRAM requirement. The README names scripts/train_real_astro_dataset.py, absent from the inspected complete tree; current scripts/train.py instead can generate synthetic training data when input images are missing. The exact bundled checkpoint provenance and SDSS-only training claim therefore remain unverified. Detection counts, plate-solving fidelity and photometric preservation need independent evaluation.

### How it uses AI

A Windows WPF sky-image workspace combines conventional photometry with optional tiled YOLO ONNX detection, mosaic alignment, a celestial dome and equirectangular panorama export. 1. Load a representative FITS/TIFF/PNG/JPEG/BMP frame or small mosaic, keeping the original files.
2. Compare Photometry, Hybrid Fusion and DirectML modes and inspect the displayed provider/model state.
3. Adjust inclusion/exclusion masks and per-photo confidence rather than trusting every proposed star.
4. Review alignment and dome view; export a mosaic/panorama or CSV/JSON and inspect the saved result. Optional Stellarium synchronization uses its HTTP Remote Control on port 8090.

- **Software:** Windows 10/11 x64. Current UI targets net10.0-windows/WPF; source builds need .NET 10 SDK. Core packages include ONNX Runtime DirectML 1.24.4, SkiaSharp and LibTiff. Stellarium is optional for live synchronization.
- **Platforms:** Windows desktop route documented. DirectML initialization and CPU fallback are present in source; neither establishes tested GPU compatibility, throughput or support on macOS/Linux.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.Core/Inference/OnnxInferenceEngine.cs) [Source 3](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.UI/AstroCounter.UI.csproj) [Source 4](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.Core/AstroCounter.Core.csproj) [Source 5](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/scripts/train.py)

### Limitations

The source uses 640×640 neural tiles, but a tile size is not a minimum VRAM requirement. The README names scripts/train_real_astro_dataset.py, absent from the inspected complete tree; current scripts/train.py instead can generate synthetic training data when input images are missing. The exact bundled checkpoint provenance and SDSS-only training claim therefore remain unverified. Detection counts, plate-solving fidelity and photometric preservation need independent evaluation. Current open reports also describe 2D constellation-line distortion and artifacts in 360 export. Inspect saved projections before using them in an immersive production. [Source 1](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.Core/Inference/OnnxInferenceEngine.cs) [Source 3](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.UI/AstroCounter.UI.csproj) [Source 4](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/src/AstroCounter.Core/AstroCounter.Core.csproj) [Source 5](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/scripts/train.py) [Source 6](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/issues/8) [Source 7](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/issues/7) [Source 8](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/issues/5)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/ghostkronos/AstroCounter/blob/main/README.md)

### threecat · MIT

Explore external-agent-authored browser-game prototypes using the current M1 basic runtime, without treating the complete engine roadmap as delivered.
A code-first TypeScript/WebGPU engine provides public SDK/CLI and observation/verification interfaces intended for an external AI coding agent to build games; the game runtime itself has no LLM.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Current docs report M1 basic-runtime acceptance, replacing the historical M0 status. The SDK/compiler, ECS, scheduling, snapshots and control bridge are documented; complete visual prefab instantiation, production rendering optimization and full character/physics/UI/audio remain later milestones. Windows setup uses Node 24 or a locally provisioned verified toolchain. Owner acceptance counts and two AI flows are not independent first-hand evaluation.

### Householder Motion Embedding / NoiseControl · Apache-2.0

Research motion transfer from video or a precomputed flow field, with the required trained adapter distinguished from unconditioned base inference.
Flow-conditioned Householder transforms convert reference optical flow into temporally correlated Gaussian initial noise for a CogVideoX image-to-video workflow.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The fixed FlowHouseholder initialization is included, but the 4.2 GB trained motion LoRA is still coming soon. Scripts fall back to the base model when it is absent, so a video alone would not prove the paper method. Apache-2.0 code, CogVideoX weights, RAFT and training-data terms remain separate.

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **ASTRA-SR** · excluded: The current full grant applies CC BY-NC 4.0 to code, weights and data. Its non-commercial software condition does not meet this project’s open-source-only eligibility. [Source](https://github.com/I2WM/ASTRA-SR)
- **openbxt** · needs-license-review: A README MIT claim was observed but the complete repository license endpoint returned 404. No complete software grant was established. [Source](https://github.com/xlisp/openbxt)
- **Text2TactileGraphics-Code** · needs-license-review: The paper and code describe tactile relief generation, but no complete software license was returned by the repository license endpoint. - **Furge / AI Fursuit Pattern Generator** · excluded: The README contains released/portable-build claims but explicitly says the app and AI step are not built yet. Its previews identify mockups, human artwork and existing FreeSewing patterns. No implemented creative AI route was established, so it is not an eligible app or lead merely because of the title. - **AnyMo — complete repository** · excluded: Original contributions are Apache-2.0, but current licensing-scope and NOTICE explicitly retain non-commercial research restrictions on SnapMoGen-derived encoder/decoder and quantizer components. [Source](https://github.com/interestingzhuo/AnyMo/blob/main/LICENSE_STATUS.md) [Source](https://github.com/interestingzhuo/AnyMo/blob/main/NOTICE)
- **PHYSARUM** · excluded: The reviewed implementation description is a procedural WebGPU slime-mold simulation and Web Audio patch. No implemented creative AI role was established; usefulness as generative art alone does not meet this project’s AI-only scope. [Source](https://github.com/Takramer/physarum)
- **Bytehoven** · excluded: The documented audiovisual output deterministically maps coding-agent activity metadata to notes/animation. It says the sonification never calls a model; an implemented AI contribution to creating the media was not established in this review. The ordinary agent-monitoring/conducting path is not enough to label it a creative AI generation tool. [Source](https://github.com/berkinozturk/bytehoven)
